AZN — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • Nothing AstraZeneca does sets the realised price of an existing patient.
  • The deductions totalled $23,941m in FY2025 against a whole-group cost of sales of $10,633m, so they are 2.25x manufacturing cost.
  • Over 2022-2025 AstraZeneca added $15,285m of US gross Product Sales and kept $6,190m of it, a 40.5% marginal net capture against a 49.5% average; in FY2025 alone the marginal capture was 26.5%.
  • Against US Oncology Product Sales compounding at 21.4% a year from 2020 to 2025, roughly 88-91% of the growth is volume and 9-12% is price.
  • US Calquence plus US Farxiga were $4,069m of FY2025 net US Product Sales, 17.4% of the US book, and both now have an administratively set price.
  • And partnered gross-profit share, at $1,875m of Oncology Total Revenue in FY2025 and the fastest-growing branch, is the dollar over which AstraZeneca has the least control: Daiichi Sankyo owns and manufactures Enhertu and Datroway, AstraZeneca books no US Enhertu Product Sales and holds no Datroway commercialisation rights.
  • Oncology and Rare Disease keep most of their list price and BioPharmaceuticals gives most of it away.
  • Every point of margin expansion has been commercial-cost dilution against a bigger revenue base; none has come from research efficiency.
  • H1 2026 is the first period in which the incremental margin rose sharply, and the mechanism is mix rather than scale.
  • Gross margin went 82% (FY2025) to 83% (H1 2026) to 84% (Q2 2026) while Oncology's share of Total Revenue went 43.6% to 46% to 48%.
competitive-advantage
  • AstraZeneca earns returns above its cost of capital today, and the advantage is a portfolio of patent-dated monopolies plus a scale capability in trial execution and global launch. It is not a structure that keeps rivals out.
  • Gross margin was 81.9% in FY2025 against a peer median of 74.8%, a 7.1-point premium that has been stable for six years and widened to 83.6% in Q2 2026 as cost of sales grew 3% CER against 5% revenue growth.
  • And Oncology now supplies all of the group's growth: 70% of group revenue growth in FY2025, 82% in H1 2026 and 109% in Q2 2026, the last of those meaning the rest of AstraZeneca shrank.
  • Calquence still grew 16% CER in Q2 2026 and crossed $1bn in a quarter for the first time, which is what zero switching cost plus a slow-decaying installed base looks like: the incumbent keeps its existing patients and loses the new ones.
  • The company's response was to license a rival molecule, paying Dizal $600m upfront plus up to $900m of milestones plus tiered royalties for Zegfrovy in July 2026, framed as "a clear signal of our intent to remain the definitive leader in this space".
  • Research and development was 24.2% of Total Revenue in FY2025, the highest of the large-cap group except Merck, Regeneron and Vertex, so Reported operating margin of 23.4% sat 5.3 points below the peer median of 28.7% and Reported return on invested capital of 15.6% sat 6.1 points below the peer median of 21.7%.
  • The barrier to displacing an incumbent molecule, once the first is cleared, is close to zero: no installed base, no data gravity, no contract that runs, no integration cost.
  • Respiratory & Immunology holds a real barrier: the inhaled portfolio is a drug-device combination, which makes generic erosion an order of magnitude slower than for a tablet.
  • Cardiovascular, Renal & Metabolism has almost no advantage left: it went from +2% CER in FY2025 to −12% CER in H1 2026 and −18% CER in Q2 2026 within one quarter of Farxiga's US loss of exclusivity, its Maximum Fair Price and a lost Chinese tender bid arriving together.
  • On the Reported basis AstraZeneca cleared a 7–8% cost of capital in eight of the last ten years and FY2025's 16.0% is the best of the decade; on the Core basis it has earned 18.6% to 23.3% in every year except FY2021.
  • On the single test that identifies a moat being competed away, AstraZeneca is on the wrong side of it, not because it got worse but because it improved less than the field.
  • A reader judging the Oncology moat off the group's 15.6% Reported return on invested capital understates it by roughly a factor of two, and a reader judging Rare Disease off its Core margin overstates its Reported contribution by roughly 23 points.
  • IFRS 3 required Alexion's inventory to be fair-valued at $6,769m against a pre-acquisition carrying value of $1,134m, a 6.0x uplift, which is only possible where cost of sales is a single-digit percentage of price.
  • For contrast, Vertex on its own numbers — 86.2% gross margin and 34.8% operating margin in FY2025 — is what a durable pharmaceutical advantage looks like. AstraZeneca does not reach those levels.
competitive-landscape
  • AstraZeneca is the second-largest oncology franchise in the industry by revenue and the only one of the top five that combines top-three scale with mid-teens growth.
  • The ranking is less stable than the level suggests: over FY2024-FY2025 Johnson & Johnson closed from 0.93x to 0.99x of AstraZeneca's oncology revenue, so the number-two and number-three positions have converged, while AstraZeneca's gap to Merck narrowed from 1.50x to 1.40x and it passed both Bristol Myers Squibb and Roche's cancer book.
  • In BioPharmaceuticals it is second by value in SGLT-2 inhibition while disclosing that Farxiga "retained its position as the number one SGLT2 inhibitor worldwide by volume", second to GSK on respiratory scale, and the owner of the leading infant respiratory-syncytial-virus molecule while capturing only part of its in-market dollar.
  • Competition happens inside mechanism-and-indication cells, and those are among the most concentrated in healthcare: the checkpoint-inhibitor class has an index above 3,600 with a single majority player and PARP inhibition has one player near 78%. The structure is a fragmented aggregate composed of concentrated monopolies and duopolies, each with a hard expiry date.
  • In China the state does: volume-based procurement is a reverse auction that assigns volume to the lowest bidder, so losing a bid removes the hospital channel rather than shaving price.
  • Imfinzi's economics rest on being the combination backbone across eleven indications, and once a cheap alternative backbone exists, every payer negotiation for an Imfinzi-containing regimen is conducted against it.
  • a rival passed AstraZeneca in a class AstraZeneca described as standard of care, and the word "Brukinsa" appears in zero of the 43 archived AstraZeneca earnings transcripts from 2015Q4 to 2026Q2, so an investor reading only AstraZeneca's disclosure would not learn that the class revenue leader changed
critical-points
  • A franchise that is the only source of growth is a concentration risk rather than a diversification benefit, and its own largest medicine, Tagrisso at $7,254m, decelerated to +6% CER from +8% in H1 2026 and +10% CER in FY2025.
  • Farxiga $8,400m, Symbicort $2,885m, Soliris $1,837m, Brilinta $823m, roxadustat $274m and FluMist $272m total $14,491m, 26.1% of FY2025 Product Sales; adding Lynparza at its 2027 US expiry gives $17,770m, 32.0%.
  • Of 80 five-year windows in a 22-company reference class starting from a revenue base above $45bn, three cleared a +6.4% forward compound rate and all three were Pfizer: two of them the same COVID window, the third the Wyeth acquisition.
  • Core operating margin was 31.7% (FY2023), 31.3% (FY2024) and 31.5% (FY2025) — unmoved for three years — against an Investor Day commitment of "mid-30s% Core operating margin by 2026".
  • Q2 2026 Reported operating profit was $3,164m against $3,508m, −10% actual and −13% CER, and Reported EPS $1.61 was −2% CER, while Core EPS rose 18% CER.
expectations
  • Consensus takes management's $80bn 2030 revenue ambition essentially at face value — $80,594m against $80bn — and then goes further than management on margin, at a 38.0% Core operating margin in FY2030 against a company commitment of "at least mid-30s%".
  • FY2025 was the first year since FY2020 with no upward revision at any quarter, and it landed at the exact centre of the revenue band and the bottom third of the EPS band.
financial-history
  • Any model taking FY2025's +8% CER growth, 23.4% Reported operating margin, +2% CER cardio-renal franchise or +4% CER China as the current state is six to twelve months stale on all four.
  • The metric that is new and growing fastest is Alliance Revenue, and it is the one an investor should watch most closely, because it is simultaneously the fastest-growing revenue line, the highest reported gross margin in the company, and the revenue over which AstraZeneca has the least commercial control.
forward-model
  • On margin the model reaches 33.6% by FY2030 and peaks at 33.7% in FY2031, which is already +2.1 points on FY2025 delivered by mix rather than scale and requires selling and administrative expense growing 3.5–4.0% against revenue growing 2–4%; consensus reaches 38.0%, which maps to roughly 32.5% Reported once amortisation is held near $4.3bn, or +900bp on the reported basis — an outcome that appears in 6 of 104 windows above $25bn of revenue and in no window above $45bn ever.
  • So the answer on this model is that the pipeline is roughly sufficient to hold revenue flat through the cliff at a 55% probability of success, and not sufficient to grow through it.
  • Oncology reaches 49.5% of Total Revenue in FY2030, against 43.6% in FY2025 and 48% in Q2 2026, then declines to 23.7% by FY2035 as the four large medicines lose protection.
secular-trends
  • So the ambition implies AstraZeneca grows with its market and holds share flat for five years, at half the 13.2% rate of the last two.
  • The correct statement is that AstraZeneca gained volume and list share and lost net-revenue share, and the gap is the rebate line.
  • Separately, AstraZeneca withdrew its per-region market-share disclosure in FY2025.
  • Peak to peak, 2012 to 2025, is a 5.7% compound rate over thirteen years, close to the 5–8% market rate and close to the 6.4% the $80bn ambition implies.
  • The 17% from the cardio-renal franchise is now reversing; it was the tail of the Farxiga cycle rather than trend.
technology
  • No technology AstraZeneca uses excludes a competitor.
  • An antibody-drug conjugate joins a targeting antibody to a cytotoxic payload through a linker; the design problem is that a linker which releases payload in circulation delivers chemotherapy to the whole body, which caps the dose.
  • The proprietary platform's first readout is dated 2027, and the $1.5bn Singapore plant is being built ahead of any wholly owned conjugate revenue.
  • Imfinzi was $6,063m of FY2025 Product Sales, so the franchise being defended and the franchise being replaced are the same one.
  • Propellant emissions are 26% of AstraZeneca's total Scope 3 footprint, so in health systems that ration by carbon footprint the transition is a commercial defence as much as a climate one — GSK's respiratory franchise is dry-powder-weighted and does not have the same problem.
  • Hypophosphatasia has no competing approved product and a population of about 11,500 diagnosed patients across the top eight countries, so the exclusion mechanism is orphan status plus the practical impossibility of recruiting a competing trial.
  • The forward revenue case does not rest on any of the transformative platforms.
value-chain
  • AstraZeneca is the largest single profit pool in its own chain by a wide margin, and the binding constraint is not capacity or input supply but the price set at the interface between AstraZeneca and the payer.
  • AstraZeneca's 11.58 cents of Reported operating profit per list dollar is roughly 10x the wholesaler's 1.1 cents and roughly 3x the pharmacy benefit manager's 3-5 cents on the flow each handles. The manufacturer has not lost its share of the chain's profit pool.
  • The 2016-to-2022 improvement of 16.3 points was mix, not bargaining power: the US portfolio moved from retail primary care (Crestor, Nexium, Symbicort, heavily rebated) to hospital and specialty medicines dispensed through infusion centres and specialty pharmacy, which rebate far less.
  • Structurally that converts ex-US price negotiations from independent negotiations into inputs to the US price, so a European or Japanese payer now has leverage over the US price.
  • The one place AstraZeneca does not control its own supply is the fastest-growing part of its revenue.

Nothing AstraZeneca does sets the realised price of an existing patient.

filingsupports ✦ best
When invoicing Product Sales in the US, we estimate the rebates and chargebacks that we expect to pay, which are considered to be estimates. These rebates typically arise from sales contracts with third-party managed care organisations, hospitals, long-term care facilities, group purchasing organisations and various federal or state programmes (Medicaid contracts, supplemental rebates, etc.). ... Accrual assumptions are built up on a product-by-product and customer-by customer basis, taking into account specific contract provisions coupled with expected performance, and are then aggregated into a weighted average rebate accrual rate for each of our products. ... Overall adjustments between gross and net US Product Sales amounted to $23,941 million in 2025 (2024: $18,986 million) with the increase driven predominantly by increased rebates from contractual arrangements and chargebacks.
— AstraZeneca PLC Form 20-F FY2025, 2026-02-24
filingsupports
The US healthcare system is complex. Multiple payers and intermediaries influence patient access to branded medicines through regulatory rebates in government programmes and voluntary rebates paid to private insurers for commercially insured patients. Significant pricing pressure is driven by payer consolidation, restrictive reimbursement policies and cost control tools which reduce patient access. In October 2025, we announced an agreement with the US administration to lower the cost of prescription medicines in America. We voluntarily agreed to a range of measures that will enable the American healthcare system and patients to access medicines at prices that are equalised with those available in other wealthy countries.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24
filingqualifies
Chargebacks, where we enter into arrangements under which certain parties, typically hospitals, long-term care facilities, group purchasing organisations, the Department of Veterans Affairs, Public Health Service Covered Entities, and the Department of Defense, are able to buy products from wholesalers at the lower prices we have contracted with them. The chargeback is the difference between the price we invoice to the wholesaler and the contracted price charged by the wholesaler to the other party. Chargebacks are credited directly to the wholesalers
— AstraZeneca PLC Form 20-F FY2025, 2026-02-24
filingqualifies
Calquence sales in the US increased by 7% to $2,339 million (2024: $2,190 million; 2023: $1,815 million), as a result of growth in new patient starts in CLL, 1L MCL (ECHO) launch and improved affordability offsetting Medicare Part D redesign and also discounts to secure preferential formulary placement.
— AstraZeneca PLC Form 20-F FY2025, 2026-02-24

In the United States (US), the Group sells to customers under various commercial and government mandated contracts and reimbursement arrangements that include rebates, chargebacks and a right of return for certain pharmaceutical products. ... The US RAR balance sheet accrual at 31 December 2025 amounted to £4,891 million. The five most significant buying groups to which the RAR accrual relates are Managed Care, Medicaid, Ryan White, Medicare Part D, and the Medicare Part D Manufacturer Discount Program.
— GSK Form 20-F FY2025, 2026-03-06

The Inflation Reduction Act (IRA) of 2022 was passed to address Medicare spending concerns. Farxiga was selected for the first round of Medicare price negotiations under the IRA. As the Maximum Fair Price for Medicare has now taken effect in 2026, coinciding in the same year as the expected US loss of market exclusivity that will also reduce Farxiga's price, the overall impact is expected to be manageable. Calquence was selected for the second round of price negotiations in 2025. Its Maximum Fair Price for Medicare will take effect in 2027. Our diversified product portfolio, providing a broad spectrum of treatments across therapy areas, well position us to mitigate business impact.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24

Calquence Inflation Reduction Act Litigation Considered to be a contingent asset US • In December 2025, AstraZeneca filed a lawsuit in the US District Court for the District of Maryland challenging the US Department of Health and Human Services' interpretation of "qualifying single source drug" under the Inflation Reduction Act and its application in selecting Calquence for drug price negotiation. Farxiga Inflation Reduction Act Litigation Considered to be a contingent asset US • In August 2023, AstraZeneca filed a lawsuit in the US District Court for the District of Delaware (District Court) against the US Department of Health and Human Services (HHS) challenging aspects of the drug price negotiation provisions of the Inflation Reduction Act and the implementing guidance and regulations. In March 2024, the District Court granted HHS' motions and dismissed AstraZeneca's lawsuit. • In May 2025, the US Court of Appeals for the Third Circuit affirmed the District Court's dismissal of AstraZeneca's challenge. • In September 2025, AstraZeneca sought review by the US Supreme Court.

filingsupports ✦ best
Enhertu and Datroway (Daiichi Sankyo) In March 2019, AstraZeneca entered into an alliance with Daiichi Sankyo to develop and commercialise Enhertu for multiple cancer types. In July 2020, AstraZeneca entered into an alliance with Daiichi Sankyo to develop and commercialise Datroway, a TROP2- directed ADC. In markets where Daiichi Sankyo is selling the products, AstraZeneca is entitled to receive a royalty (in Japan) or a share of costs and income (in other territories). Share of gross profits and royalty income from Daiichi Sankyo are recognised as Alliance Revenue. Enhertu launched in the US in December 2019. Datroway launched in Japan in March 2025.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24
filingsupports
Joint contractual arrangements were entered into between AstraZeneca and Daiichi Sankyo, Inc. (Daiichi Sankyo); in March 2019 for the co-development and co-commercialisation of Enhertu and in July 2020 for the co-development and co-commercialisation of Datroway. Each party shares global pre-tax net income from the collaboration on a 50:50 basis (with the exception of Japan where Daiichi Sankyo maintains exclusive rights and AstraZeneca receives a royalty). The joint operation is not structured through a separate legal entity, and it operates from AstraZeneca and Daiichi Sankyo's respective principal places of business.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24

Dizal Pharmaceutical Co In July 2026, AstraZeneca entered into an exclusive license agreement with Dizal Pharmaceutical Co (Dizal), Ltd for Zegfrovy (sunvozertinib), a novel oral irreversible EGFR inhibitor for patients with lung cancer. AstraZeneca will acquire worldwide rights to develop and commercialise Zegfrovy, which is approved in the US and China for the treatment of adult patients with locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with EGFR exon 20 insertion mutations, whose disease has progressed on or after platinum-based chemotherapy. AstraZeneca will make an upfront payment to Dizal of $600m and additional payments of up to $900m upon achievement of specific development, regulatory and sales-related milestones. Additionally, Dizal will receive tiered royalties on the global sales of Zegfrovy. The transaction is expected to close in the second half of 2026, subject to customary closing conditions and regulatory clearances.
— AstraZeneca PLC, H1 and Q2 2026 results announcement (Form 6-K), 2026-07-27

Competition happens inside mechanism-and-indication cells, and those are among the most concentrated in healthcare: the checkpoint-inhibitor class has an index above 3,600 with a single majority player and PARP inhibition has one player near 78%. The structure is a fragmented aggregate composed of concentrated monopolies and duopolies, each with a hard expiry date.

Peer: Merck & Co.

filingsupports
While pricing pressure has always existed in China, health care reform has increased this pressure in part due to the acceleration of generic substitution through volume-based procurement (VBP). The government has implemented the VBP program through a tendering process for mature products which have generic substitutes with a Generic Quality Consistency Evaluation approval. Mature products that have entered into the latest rounds of VBP had, on average, a price reduction of more than 50%. The Company expects that the VBP process will have a significant impact on mature products moving forward.
— Merck & Co. Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, Risk Factors - China, 2026-02-24

Peer: Sanofi

filingsupports ✦ best
In China, the authorities have implemented a range of healthcare cost containment measures, including the Volume Based Procurement (VBP) reverse auction that particularly impacts our insulin-based products, Plavix, Aprovel, and Lovenox. A large number of molecules were selected to submit tenders under successive waves of the VBP program, with the successful bidders being awarded a high level of market share in return for offering lower prices. The recent eleventh round of VBP results remained very unfavorable to multinational companies. Domestic generic companies confirmed the existing trend, and won the main bids due to further aggressive price reductions.

**(b) Only one company ever cleared the bar, and it took a pandemic.** The three windows out of 80 with a forward-5yr CAGR ≥ +6.4% from a ≥$45bn base: | Company | Start FY | Start revenue (USDm) | Forward 5yr CAGR | What drove it | |---|---|---|---|---| | Pfizer | 2017 | 52,546 | +13.8% | FY2022 revenue $100,330m — Comirnaty + Paxlovid | | Pfizer | 2016 | 52,824 | +9.0% | same COVID window (ends FY2021) | | Pfizer | 2005 | 47,405 | +7.2% | Wyeth acquisition (2009, $68bn) | Both non-COVID members of that list are M&A-driven, and Pfizer's COVID revenue subsequently reversed (FY2025 $62,579m, below the FY2022 peak by 38%).
— AZN deep-dive workpaper: expectations-baserate (reference class of 22 pharmaceutical companies, Bloomberg annual fundamentals FY1994-FY2025), 2026-08-07
filingsupports
**(c) Over ten years nobody has done it.** Of 57 ten-year windows starting from ≥$45bn, the maximum realized CAGR is +6.3% and the median is +1.8%. Zero cleared +6.4%. AstraZeneca's ambition is a five-year requirement, but the "and sustained ... Momentum matters below $45bn and stops mattering above it. This is the single most important number in this workpaper: **at AstraZeneca's scale, starting from AstraZeneca's growth rate, 1 in 24 historical windows delivered what the ambition requires, and that one was Pfizer into COVID.**
— AZN deep-dive workpaper: expectations-baserate (reference class of 22 pharmaceutical companies, Bloomberg annual fundamentals FY1994-FY2025), 2026-08-07
filingqualifies
The ambition asks for **$21,261m of net new revenue in five years** (consensus asks $21,855m). Distribution of absolute revenue added over five years from a ≥$45bn base (n=80): - median **+$3,223m**; p75 +$9,223m; p90 +$15,398m; max +$47,784m - windows adding ≥$21,261m: **3 of 80 (3.8%)** — Pfizer FY2017 (+$47,784m), Pfizer FY2016 (+$28,464m), J&J FY2016 (+$21,885m, and J&J's addition is partly MedTech).
— AZN deep-dive workpaper: expectations-baserate (reference class of 22 pharmaceutical companies, Bloomberg annual fundamentals FY1994-FY2025), 2026-08-07
filingqualifies
1. **J&J, Bayer, Sanofi and Merck KGaA are diversified.** J&J's revenue includes MedTech; Bayer's includes Crop Science; Merck KGaA's includes Life Science and Electronics. Their growth is not a pure innovative-pharma read. Excluding them from the ≥$45bn screen removes 41 of 80 five-year windows and does not change the conclusion (the three windows that cleared +6.4% are all Pfizer). 2. **Total revenue includes M&A.** Every base rate below is a TOTAL revenue base rate, so acquisitions count. AstraZeneca's own $80bn ambition explicitly does **not**: the Investor Day footnote states it is "not contingent upon future M&A. Based on Q1 2024 exchange rates" (FY2025 results deck slide 28; `archive\AZN\decks\slides\AZN_2026-02-10_fy-and-q4-2025-results\slide_28.txt`). The base rate is therefore **generous** to the ambition, not harsh: it credits peers for growth AstraZeneca has said it will not buy.
— AZN deep-dive workpaper: expectations-baserate (reference class of 22 pharmaceutical companies, Bloomberg annual fundamentals FY1994-FY2025), 2026-08-07
filingqualifies
Built from Bloomberg annual fundamentals on a FISCAL basis, FY1994-FY2025, 22 companies, 5,664 field-year observations (`baserate_peer_annual_raw.csv`, `baserate_peer_panel.csv`; archived at `archive\AZN\market\baserate_2026-08-07\`). Pull script `_br_pull.py`, analysis `_br_analyze.py`, `_br_analyze2.py`, `_br_final.py`. ... | Biogen | BIIB US | USD | 9,891 | 9,891 | the franchise-erosion case study | Growth and margin statistics are computed **in each company's own reporting currency** so FX does not contaminate them. The USD column exists only to apply the size screen; it uses year-end spot from Bloomberg (`baserate_fx_annual.csv`), not annual averages, so treat the USD level as ±3% `[estimate: year-end spot FX]`.

Core R&D expense increased by 6% in the first half, reflecting continued investment in our pipeline. Following the positive Phase IIb results for oral GLP-1 molecule, elecoglipron, we have now initiated comprehensive Phase III program in both obesity and type 2 diabetes with first patients dosed earlier this month. Core R&D represented 23% of total revenue in the first half and we continue to expect R&D expenses to be at the upper end of the low 20s percentage range for the full year as we continue to build our pipeline for long-term growth opportunities, including bispecifics, cell therapies, engagers in addition to our CVRM portfolio. ... filingsupports
Effective 1 January 2025, the Group has updated the presentation of Total Revenue on the face of the Statement of Comprehensive Income to include a new subtotal 'Product Revenue' representing the summation of Product Sales and Alliance Revenue. ... Product Sales and Alliance Revenue will continue to be presented separately, with the new subtotal providing additional aggregation of revenue types with similar characteristics, reflecting the growing importance of Alliance Revenue. ... Effective 1 January 2025, the Group has replaced the measure of 'Product Sales Gross Margin' with the measure of 'Gross Margin'. Previously, the measure excluded margin related to Alliance Revenue and Collaboration Revenue. The new measure is calculated using Gross profit as a percentage of Total Revenue, thereby encompassing all revenue categories, and is intended to provide a more comprehensive measure of total performance.
— AstraZeneca results: FY and Q4 2025 (Form 6-K), 'Reporting changes since FY 2024', 2026-02-10
filingsupports
The Group periodically enters into transactions where it acquires part of the rights to a product intangible (either on-market or in-process R&D), but for commercial reasons does not act as principal in selling the product to the customer and therefore does not recognise income from the product in the form of Product Sales. This may occur where, for example, a collaboration partner retains the right to commercialise in a specific territory, and has sufficient local control over that commercialisation to book Product Sales, while the Group instead receives a proportion of the value generated by those Product Sales, either in the form of a share of gross profits, a share of revenues or a royalty.
— AstraZeneca Form 20-F FY2025, Note 1 Basis of preparation and accounting policies - Revenue, 2026-02-24
filingsupports
Alliance Revenue comprises income arising from the ongoing operation of collaborative arrangements related to sales made by collaboration partners, where AstraZeneca is entitled to a share of gross profits, a share of revenues or royalties, which are recurring in nature while the collaboration agreement remains in place. Alliance Revenue does not include Product Sales where AstraZeneca is leading commercialisation in a territory, or reimbursement for AstraZeneca-incurred expenses such as R&D or promotion costs, which arise from the license of intellectual property.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Exhibit 15.1), Note 1 - Alliance Revenue, 2026-02-24

Intellectual property rights are essential to sustaining the incentives our industry needs to invest in R&D that leads to new medicines. Drug development is inherently long, uncertain and costly, particularly when accounting for the high rate of failure. Early in R&D, thousands, and in some areas millions, of compounds may be screened to identify the few with the potential to become safe, effective therapies and ultimately secure regulatory approval. A robust system for obtaining, maintaining and enforcing patents is a critical pillar of a sustainable innovation ecosystem.

These technologies will deliver products that will be very difficult to copy rapidly by generic companies. An antibody-drug conjugate joins a targeting antibody to a cytotoxic payload through a linker; the design problem is that a linker which releases payload in circulation delivers chemotherapy to the whole body, which caps the dose.

presentationsupports ✦ best
And then in terms of the specificity for use with Dato-DXd, I've emphasized in the prepared remarks that I made and I'll emphasize again there, I think Dato-DXd is differentiated because of its highly plasma-stable linker, and therefore a higher proportion of its activity is dependent on the internalization and then the subsequent cleavage within the cytoplasm of that linker and release of the payload. That is different from something where you've got a very short half-life, a nonstable linker and just basically a lot of high exposure to free payload. And you see this not just with the half-life, but with the difference in the bone marrow toxicity that you see. Bone marrow toxicity is driven through exposure to free payload typically.
— Susan Galbraith, EVP Oncology Haematology R&D, AstraZeneca PLC Special Call (WCLC 2024 investor event, TROPION-Lung01 and QCS), 2024-09-09
presentationsupports
What's different about Dato-DXd is it's got a highly plasma-stable linker. This is supported by its half-life of 5 days compared to other TROP2 ADCs that have less than 1 day half-life, and so you're just getting exposure to free payload. Dato-DXd, differently, binds to the membrane TROP2 and then needs to be internalized for its cytotoxic payload to be released. So because not all of the TROP2 protein on the cell surface ends up in the cytoplasm, you need this assessment on both the cell membrane and the cytoplasm to better predict the response to Dato-DXd.
— Susan Galbraith, EVP Oncology Haematology R&D, AstraZeneca PLC Special Call (WCLC 2024 investor event, TROPION-Lung01 and QCS), 2024-09-09
presentationsupports
And the reason for this is that TROP2 is a highly post-translationally modified protein that actually undergoes cleavage. Not everything that you see and that you can stain on the outside of the cell is relevant to the mechanism of action of an ADC that depends on internalization. So because some of it is cleaved, what you need to look at is the fraction of the overall surface expression that ends up in the cytoplasm. And this also points to the differentiated mechanism of action of Dato-DXd versus other ADCs.
— Susan Galbraith, EVP Oncology Haematology R&D, AstraZeneca PLC Special Call (WCLC 2024 investor event, TROPION-Lung01 and QCS), 2024-09-09

presentationsupports
Volrustomig is our PD-1 CTLA-4 bispecific. We know that CTLA-4 inhibition can improve long-term survival. It can improve that tail on the survival curve, but comes with a toxicity profile, which is limited the use currently -- anti-CTLA-4 antibodies. This molecule is designed to minimize those toxicities and deliver full inhibition of PD-1 at levels of CTLA-4 inhibition which go beyond what is currently available with either temelimab or ipilimumab.

We remain committed to Ambition Zero Carbon and the reduction of value chain emissions. The 2026 PSP builds on the approach to Scope 3 emissions reduction introduced in the 2025 PSP, with the Sustainability metric for the 2026 PSP focusing on the NGP transition. This reflects the NGP's material impact on Scope 3 carbon emissions (26% of total emissions in 2025) and its status as the largest device transition in AstraZeneca's history.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report & Form 20-F Information 2025 (Exhibit 15.1), Directors' Remuneration Report — 2026 Performance Share Plan measures, 2026-02-24
filingsupports
As part of our efforts to provide patients with access to treatment with lower GHG emissions, we are transitioning our portfolio of inhaled respiratory medicines delivered by pMDIs to use a NGP with near-zero GWP. pMDIs deliver essential, life-saving medicines for millions of people living with respiratory diseases worldwide and are the most commonly used type of inhaler device globally. Our NGP has 99.9% less GWP than propellants used in our current pMDI portfolio, which makes the transition to the NGP a key product-related element of our Ambition Zero Carbon strategy.

HPP is a rare, inherited and progressive metabolic disease characterised by defective mineralisation (the process that hardens and strengthens bones and teeth), impaired calcium and phosphate regulation, and non-skeletal manifestations such as muscle weakness, generalised fatigue and pain. So let me first talk about hypophosphatasia. Hypophosphatasia is a disease caused by a deficiency in the enzyme alkaline phosphatase and the cause of that deficiency is extreme impaired bone formation. In neonatal, it can be absolutely fatal. ... Strensiq has been on the market since 2015. It has reached blockbuster status in 2023. The product is progressing very well.

presentationqualifies

We have 3 clinical trials, which are conducted globally, and the data are anticipated post 2025.

In October 2025, we announced an agreement with the US administration to lower the cost of prescription medicines in America. We voluntarily agreed to a range of measures that will enable the American healthcare system and patients to access medicines at prices that are equalised with those available in other wealthy countries. We also reached an agreement with the US Department of Commerce for a three-year exemption … of Section 232 tariffs on medicines imported to the US, enabling the Group to onshore medicines manufacturing so that substantially all of its medicines sold in the US are made in the US.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F Exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24

Executive Order 14297, “Delivering Most-Favored-Nation Prescription Drug Pricing to American Patients,” issued in May 2025, seeks to facilitate manufacturers’ sale of certain drugs in the U.S. at no higher than the lowest prices paid in other developed countries. It also directs HHS to enable direct-to-consumer purchasing programs for prescription drugs at most-favored-nation prices, which may bypass supply chain intermediaries.
— McKesson Corporation Form 10-K for the fiscal year ended March 31, 2026, 2026-05-08
filingsupports

filingsupports
The Group periodically enters into transactions where it acquires part of the rights to a product intangible (either on-market or in-process R&D), but for commercial reasons does not act as principal in selling the product to the customer and therefore does not recognise income from the product in the form of Product Sales. This may occur where, for example, a collaboration partner retains the right to commercialise in a specific territory, and has sufficient local control over that commercialisation to book Product Sales, while the Group instead receives a proportion of the value generated by those Product Sales, either in the form of a share of gross profits, a share of revenues or a royalty.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F Exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24
filingsupports
In March 2019, AstraZeneca entered into an alliance with Daiichi Sankyo to develop and commercialise Enhertu for multiple cancer types. In July 2020, AstraZeneca entered into an alliance with Daiichi Sankyo to develop and commercialise Datroway, a TROP2- directed ADC. In markets where Daiichi Sankyo is selling the products, AstraZeneca is entitled to receive a royalty (in Japan) or a share of costs and income (in other territories). Share of gross profits and royalty income from Daiichi Sankyo are recognised as Alliance Revenue.
— AstraZeneca Annual Report and Form 20-F Information 2025 (Form 20-F Exhibit 15.1), 2026-02-24
